========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I guess I'm here because... ever since my daughter left for basic training, I just feel like I come home to a dead house. She's down at Fort Jackson right now. You know, people call it "Relaxin' Jackson" but she says the humidity is just awful down there. I went to Myrtle Beach once when I was a teenager, and the heat in South Carolina is honestly no joke, it just ruins your hair. Not that I worry about my hair much at the shop with the helmet on, but... 

Anyway, I just can’t keep myself together lately. I'm literally sitting there crying in my truck before my shifts even start. It's an '08 F-150, and the transmission is starting to slip on it, which is a whole other headache I don't want to deal with right now. My buddy Dave said he'd look at it, but he's always busy. 

But yeah, at work, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I’ve done for years. It's bad. And I keep snapping at the younger guys on the crew. Though, to be fair, these kids today are always looking at their phones, right? One of them almost dropped a sheet of 16-gauge steel on his foot last week because he was watching a video. But I know it's my temper that's the problem right now. I'm just so off. I've even stopped going out on the water, and I haven't worked on my metal sculptures at home at all. I used to love making things out of scrap, but I just don’t see the point anymore.

It's been about a month since she left. Yeah, right around four weeks now that I've been just... struggling like this. I dropped her off at the recruiter's office on a Tuesday, I remember because it was raining so hard we almost had to pull over on the interstate. The wipers on that F-150 just couldn't keep up. I don't know, it's... I guess it's a little bit of both? Like I said, I haven't been working on my sculptures at all. I used to go out to the garage every weekend and just weld scrap pieces together. Last month I was working on this heron made out of old spark plugs and rebar. It was actually turning out really good. I get the spark plugs from my neighbor, Jim. He runs a small engine repair business out of his shed. You wouldn't believe how many lawnmowers people just throw away because they don't want to clean a carburetor. Jim fixes them up and sells them on Marketplace. He made like eight hundred bucks last spring just on riding mowers. He's been trying to get me to buy one, but my yard isn't even that big, you know? Just a little patch of grass in the back that my dog mostly tears up anyway. He's a terrier mix, crazy amount of energy. But yeah, I just... I go out to the garage, look at the welding torch, and I just feel so tired. I don't even want to turn it on. I just end up going back inside and sitting on the couch.

I dropped her off at the recruiter's office on a Tuesday, I remember because it was raining so hard we almost had to pull over on the interstate. The wipers on that F-150 just couldn't keep up. I've tried changing the blades, you know, bought those expensive silicone ones from AutoZone that are supposed to last a year, but they still streak on the driver's side. It's so annoying when you're trying to see the road glare at night. But yeah, ever since that day she left, I've just been a mess. I haven't even gone to the grocery store for real food, just been living off those frozen dinners. The Salisbury steak ones aren't terrible if you put enough hot sauce on them, but it's not the same as cooking. I used to make these great pork chops... but yeah, it's been about a month.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer enjoying things that you used to enjoy, or is it just that you don't have the energy to do them?\n\nPatient: I don't know, it's... I guess it's a little bit of both? Like I said, I haven't been working on my sculptures at all. I used to go out to the garage every weekend and just weld scrap pieces together. Last month I was working on this heron made out of old spark plugs and rebar. It was actually turning out really good. I get the spark plugs from my neighbor, Jim. He runs a small engine repair business out of his shed. You wouldn't believe how many lawnmowers people just throw away because they don't want to clean a carburetor. Jim fixes them up and sells them on Marketplace. He made like eight hundred bucks last spring just on riding mowers. He's been trying to get me to buy one, but my yard isn't even that big, you know? Just a little patch of grass in the back that my dog mostly tears up anyway. He's a terrier mix, crazy amount of energy. But yeah, I just... I go out to the garage, look at the welding torch, and I just feel so tired. I don't even want to turn it on. I just end up going back inside and sitting on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your daughter leaving for basic training?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm proud of her. She's doing something important, you know? The military is a solid career path. My uncle was in the Navy, actually. He was stationed out in San Diego back in the eighties. He always talked about how beautiful the weather was out there, like seventy degrees and sunny every single day. I've always wanted to go to California, maybe see the Redwoods or drive up the Pacific Coast Highway. I saw this documentary once about how those trees can live for thousands of years, it's just wild to think about. But anyway, no, I don't feel guilty. I just miss her so much it physically hurts. It's just me and the dog now, and he's not exactly great at conversation, unless you count barking at the mailman every morning at 10 AM sharp. The new mail carrier is this young guy who always wears shorts, even when it's freezing out. I don't know how his legs don't turn purple. But yeah, no guilt, just... just this heavy feeling all the time.\n\n[Last Question]\nAre you having trouble doing your job as a welder because of how you're feeling?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 38 / sheet_metal_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"My supervisor took me off a complex welding job because I wasn’t coordinating safely enough, and I let a sculpture submission pass without finishing it.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, actually... it has been bad at the shop. My supervisor ended up taking me off this really complex welding job we were doing because he said I wasn't coordinating safely enough. Which, I mean, safety is a huge deal for us. We have this big whiteboard in the breakroom tracking our days without an incident. It was up to like two hundred days until Dave dropped a heavy piece of ductwork on his foot last month. He wasn't wearing his steel-toed boots, which is ridiculous because there's a Red Wing store right down the street that gives us a union discount. I always buy the lace-up ones, even though they take forever to break in. I usually have to wear two pairs of thick wool socks for the first week just to stop the blisters from forming on my heels. My grandma used to knit these amazing wool socks, she'd make them in all these crazy neon colors so you couldn't lose them in the laundry. I still have a pair that's bright orange, though they're getting a little threadbare at the toes now. 

But, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, they took me off that job. And it's not just at work, either. I had this deadline for a sculpture submission for a local gallery, and I just... I just let it pass. I didn't even finish the piece. The gallery is right next to this amazing Thai place, actually. They make the best drunken noodles, though the last time I went, they made it so spicy I was practically sweating through my shirt. The guy who owns it is always trying to give me free spring rolls when I pick up my takeout. But yeah... I just couldn't bring myself to finish the sculpture, and I just can't seem to focus on the shop floor right now.

I guess it's a little bit of both? Like I said, I haven't been working on my sculptures at all. I used to go out to the garage every weekend and just weld scrap pieces together. Last month I was working on this heron made out of old spark plugs and rebar. It was actually turning out really good. I get the spark plugs from my neighbor, Jim. He runs a small engine repair business out of his shed. You wouldn't believe how many lawnmowers people just throw away because they don't want to clean a carburetor. Jim fixes them up and sells them on Marketplace. He made like eight hundred bucks last spring just on riding mowers. He's been trying to get me to buy one, but my yard isn't even that big, you know? I mean, I'm proud of her. She's doing something important, you know? The military is a solid career path. My uncle was in the Navy, actually. He was stationed out in San Diego back in the eighties. He always talked about how beautiful the weather was out there, like seventy degrees and sunny every single day. I've always wanted to go to California, maybe see the Redwoods or drive up the Pacific Coast Highway. I saw this documentary once about how those trees can live for thousands of years, it's just wild to think about. But anyway, no, I don't feel guilty. I just miss her so much it physically hurts. It's just me and the dog now, and he's not exactly great at conversation, unless you count barking at the mailman every morning at 10 AM sharp. The new mail carrier is this young guy who always wears shorts, even when it's freezing out. I don't know how his legs don't turn purple. But yeah, no guilt, just... just this heavy feeling all the time.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your job as a welder because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... it has been bad at the shop. My supervisor ended up taking me off this really complex welding job we were doing because he said I wasn't coordinating safely enough. Which, I mean, safety is a huge deal for us. We have this big whiteboard in the breakroom tracking our days without an incident. It was up to like two hundred days until Dave dropped a heavy piece of ductwork on his foot last month. He wasn't wearing his steel-toed boots, which is ridiculous because there's a Red Wing store right down the street that gives us a union discount. I always buy the lace-up ones, even though they take forever to break in. I usually have to wear two pairs of thick wool socks for the first week just to stop the blisters from forming on my heels. My grandma used to knit these amazing wool socks, she'd make them in all these crazy neon colors so you couldn't lose them in the laundry. I still have a pair that's bright orange, though they're getting a little threadbare at the toes now. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, they took me off that job. And it's not just at work, either. I had this deadline for a sculpture submission for a local gallery, and I just... I just let it pass. I didn't even finish the piece. The gallery is right next to this amazing Thai place, actually. They make the best drunken noodles, though the last time I went, they made it so spicy I was practically sweating through my shirt. The guy who owns it is always trying to give me free spring rolls when I pick up my takeout. But yeah... Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, I kind of talked about that a minute ago, right? I don't really know if it's that I've completely lost interest forever, it's just... I look at the tools and I just feel nothing. Usually, I'd at least go out there and turn on the radio. I have this old boombox in the garage that actually still plays cassette tapes. I used to make mixtapes all the time back in high school. You know, sitting by the stereo for hours just waiting for a specific song to come on the radio so you could hit record at the exact right second? I had this one tape that was all Aerosmith and Def Leppard. My older brother actually stole it from me when he bought his first car, this completely rusted-out Chevy Cavalier. The muffler on that thing was so bad it sounded like a jet engine taking off in our driveway. The neighbors actually called the cops on him once because he came home at two in the morning. The cop who showed up was actually a guy he played varsity football with, which is funny, small world, right? But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the sculptures and stuff. Yeah, I just... I don't know. I just don't really care about doing any of it right now.

No, I think I'm doing okay with that stuff. I mean, I eat when I'm hungry. I've been making a lot of sandwiches lately. It's just easy, you know? Just throw some turkey and cheese on bread. He had this old calendar on the wall from 1994 with vintage cars on it that he just refused to take down. But yeah, eating and sleeping, I'm getting by fine. I sleep okay, mostly. Sad? Um... I don't really know if sad is the right word. It's more just like this heavy feeling, I guess? Like I'm just dragging. I felt kind of like this when my dog died a few years ago. He was a golden retriever mix named Buster. We got him from the shelter when he was already like five years old, so he was already pretty mellow. He used to just lay under the kitchen table while I cooked. I make a really good chili, by the way. It's my dad's recipe, he always put a little bit of dark chocolate in it to cut the acidity of the tomatoes. Sounds weird, but it totally works. He won a chili cook-off at his church with it back in, like, 2012. They gave him this incredibly tacky trophy shaped like a giant chili pepper wearing a sombrero. He kept it on the mantelpiece right next to my high school graduation photo, which I always hated because I had these awful braces with rubber bands that I chose in neon green because I thought I was being edgy. I spent so much time at the orthodontist's office, Dr. Miller. He had this massive fish tank in the waiting room with these huge angelfish... 

Wait, what did you ask me? Sadness? Yeah, I don't know, I just... I'm just not feeling like myself, but I wouldn't say I'm crying all day or anything. Just heavy.

Jim fixes them up and sells them on Marketplace. He made like eight hundred bucks last spring just on riding mowers. He's been trying to get me to buy one, but my yard isn't even that big, you know? Just a little patch of grass in the back that my dog mostly tears up anyway. He's a terrier mix, crazy amount of energy. But yeah, I just... I go out to the garage, look at the welding torch, and I just feel so tired. I don't even want to turn it on. I just end up going back inside and sitting on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your daughter leaving for basic training?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm proud of her. She's doing something important, you know? The military is a solid career path. My uncle was in the Navy, actually. He was stationed out in San Diego back in the eighties. He always talked about how beautiful the weather was out there, like seventy degrees and sunny every single day. I've always wanted to go to California, maybe see the Redwoods or drive up the Pacific Coast Highway. I saw this documentary once about how those trees can live for thousands of years, it's just wild to think about. But anyway, no, I don't feel guilty. I just miss her so much it physically hurts. It's just me and the dog now, and he's not exactly great at conversation, unless you count barking at the mailman every morning at 10 AM sharp. The new mail carrier is this young guy who always wears shorts, even when it's freezing out. I don't know how his legs don't turn purple. But yeah, no guilt, just... just this heavy feeling all the time.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your job as a welder because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... it has been bad at the shop. My supervisor ended up taking me off this really complex welding job we were doing because he said I wasn't coordinating safely enough. Which, I mean, safety is a huge deal for us. We have this big whiteboard in the breakroom tracking our days without an incident. It was up to like two hundred days until Dave dropped a heavy piece of ductwork on his foot last month. He wasn't wearing his steel-toed boots, which is ridiculous because there's a Red Wing store right down the street that gives us a union discount. I always buy the lace-up ones, even though they take forever to break in. I usually have to wear two pairs of thick wool socks for the first week just to stop the blisters from forming on my heels. My grandma used to knit these amazing wool socks, she'd make them in all these crazy neon colors so you couldn't lose them in the laundry. I still have a pair that's bright orange, though they're getting a little threadbare at the toes now. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, they took me off that job. And it's not just at work, either. I had this deadline for a sculpture submission for a local gallery, and I just... I just let it pass. I didn't even finish the piece. The gallery is right next to this amazing Thai place, actually. They make the best drunken noodles, though the last time I went, they made it so spicy I was practically sweating through my shirt. The guy who owns it is always trying to give me free spring rolls when I pick up my takeout. But yeah... I just couldn't bring myself to finish the sculpture, and I just can't seem to focus on the shop floor right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you no longer interested in doing things you used to enjoy, like welding and making sculptures?\n\nPatient: I mean, I kind of talked about that a minute ago, right? I don't really know if it's that I've completely lost interest forever, it's just... I look at the tools and I just feel nothing. Usually, I'd at least go out there and turn on the radio. I have this old boombox in the garage that actually still plays cassette tapes. I used to make mixtapes all the time back in high school. You know, sitting by the stereo for hours just waiting for a specific song to come on the radio so you could hit record at the exact right second? I had this one tape that was all Aerosmith and Def Leppard. My older brother actually stole it from me when he bought his first car, this completely rusted-out Chevy Cavalier. The muffler on that thing was so bad it sounded like a jet engine taking off in our driveway. The neighbors actually called the cops on him once because he came home at two in the morning. The cop who showed up was actually a guy he played varsity football with, which is funny, small world, right? But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the sculptures and stuff. Yeah, I just... I don't know. I just don't really care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty taking care of your basic needs, like eating or sleeping, because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I think I'm doing okay with that stuff. I mean, I eat when I'm hungry. I've been making a lot of sandwiches lately. It's just easy, you know? Just throw some turkey and cheese on bread. I actually found this really good sourdough at the bakery downtown. The one that used to be a shoe repair place? They kept the old neon sign in the window, shaped like a giant boot. I always thought that was a neat touch. The guy who runs the bakery, his name is Paul, he's got this massive handlebar mustache. It's perfectly curled at the ends, like an old-timey villain. He said he uses some special wax he orders from Germany. My grandfather had a mustache like that, but he never waxed it. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how hard he scrubbed them with that orange pumice soap. I love the smell of that soap, it always reminds me of his garage. He had this old calendar on the wall from 1994 with vintage cars on it that he just refused to take down. But yeah, eating and sleeping, I'm getting by fine. I sleep okay, mostly. Just, you know, tired a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... I don't really know if sad is the right word. It's more just like this heavy feeling, I guess? Like I'm just dragging. I felt kind of like this when my dog died a few years ago. He was a golden retriever mix named Buster. We got him from the shelter when he was already like five years old, so he was already pretty mellow. He used to just lay under the kitchen table while I cooked. I make a really good chili, by the way. It's my dad's recipe, he always put a little bit of dark chocolate in it to cut the acidity of the tomatoes. Sounds weird, but it totally works. He won a chili cook-off at his church with it back in, like, 2012. They gave him this incredibly tacky trophy shaped like a giant chili pepper wearing a sombrero. He kept it on the mantelpiece right next to my high school graduation photo, which I always hated because I had these awful braces with rubber bands that I chose in neon green because I thought I was being edgy. I spent so much time at the orthodontist's office, Dr. Miller. He had this massive fish tank in the waiting room with these huge angelfish... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Sadness? Yeah, I don't know, I just... I'm just not feeling like myself, but I wouldn't say I'm crying all day or anything. Just heavy.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to it like that. I don't really track it, you know? I was actually trying to figure that out yesterday when I was at the shop. I guess it's a little bit of both? Like I said, I haven't been working on my sculptures at all. I used to go out to the garage every weekend and just weld scrap pieces together. Just a little patch of grass in the back that my dog mostly tears up anyway. He's a terrier mix, crazy amount of energy. But yeah, I just... I go out to the garage, look at the welding torch, and I just feel so tired. I don't even want to turn it on. I just end up going back inside and sitting on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your daughter leaving for basic training?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm proud of her. She's doing something important, you know? The military is a solid career path. My uncle was in the Navy, actually. He was stationed out in San Diego back in the eighties. He always talked about how beautiful the weather was out there, like seventy degrees and sunny every single day. I've always wanted to go to California, maybe see the Redwoods or drive up the Pacific Coast Highway. I saw this documentary once about how those trees can live for thousands of years, it's just wild to think about. But anyway, no, I don't feel guilty. I just miss her so much it physically hurts. I always buy the lace-up ones, even though they take forever to break in. I usually have to wear two pairs of thick wool socks for the first week just to stop the blisters from forming on my heels. My grandma used to knit these amazing wool socks, she'd make them in all these crazy neon colors so you couldn't lose them in the laundry. I still have a pair that's bright orange, though they're getting a little threadbare at the toes now. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, they took me off that job. And it's not just at work, either. I had this deadline for a sculpture submission for a local gallery, and I just... I just let it pass. I didn't even finish the piece. The gallery is right next to this amazing Thai place, actually. They make the best drunken noodles, though the last time I went, they made it so spicy I was practically sweating through my shirt. The guy who owns it is always trying to give me free spring rolls when I pick up my takeout. But yeah... I just couldn't bring myself to finish the sculpture, and I just can't seem to focus on the shop floor right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you no longer interested in doing things you used to enjoy, like welding and making sculptures?\n\nPatient: I mean, I kind of talked about that a minute ago, right? I don't really know if it's that I've completely lost interest forever, it's just... I look at the tools and I just feel nothing. Usually, I'd at least go out there and turn on the radio. I have this old boombox in the garage that actually still plays cassette tapes. I used to make mixtapes all the time back in high school. You know, sitting by the stereo for hours just waiting for a specific song to come on the radio so you could hit record at the exact right second? I had this one tape that was all Aerosmith and Def Leppard. My older brother actually stole it from me when he bought his first car, this completely rusted-out Chevy Cavalier. The muffler on that thing was so bad it sounded like a jet engine taking off in our driveway. The neighbors actually called the cops on him once because he came home at two in the morning. The cop who showed up was actually a guy he played varsity football with, which is funny, small world, right? But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the sculptures and stuff. Yeah, I just... I don't know. I mean, I eat when I'm hungry. I've been making a lot of sandwiches lately. It's just easy, you know? Just throw some turkey and cheese on bread. I actually found this really good sourdough at the bakery downtown. The one that used to be a shoe repair place? They kept the old neon sign in the window, shaped like a giant boot. I always thought that was a neat touch. The guy who runs the bakery, his name is Paul, he's got this massive handlebar mustache. It's perfectly curled at the ends, like an old-timey villain. He said he uses some special wax he orders from Germany. My grandfather had a mustache like that, but he never waxed it. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how hard he scrubbed them with that orange pumice soap. I love the smell of that soap, it always reminds me of his garage. He had this old calendar on the wall from 1994 with vintage cars on it that he just refused to take down. But yeah, eating and sleeping, I'm getting by fine. I sleep okay, mostly. My uncle used to make his own venison jerky in this giant dehydrator he kept down in his basement. The whole house would smell like hickory smoke and black pepper for days. He was a huge hunter, had all these taxidermy deer heads mounted on the wood paneling in his den. They used to completely freak my younger cousins out when we'd go over there for Thanksgiving... \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Oh, if the heavy feeling lifts. Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you. I just haven't really noticed it going away.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you ever have days where you don't feel this heavy feeling at all?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 38 / sheet_metal_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Days where I don't feel it at all? Um... no, I don't think so. It's just kind of always there, dragging me down. Like I wake up and it's already sitting on my chest. I actually woke up super early this morning, like 4:00 AM, because the garbage truck was making this horrendous grinding noise outside my apartment. They usually don't come until Thursday, but I guess because of the holiday on Monday their whole schedule is messed up. I hate when schedules get messed up. It reminds me of when I was in high school and they changed the bell schedule halfway through the year to add this weird advisory period. I got lost on the way to my locker like three times the first week. My locker was right next to the cafeteria, so it always smelled like tater tots and bleach. They served these square pizzas on Fridays that were basically cardboard with cheese, but everybody loved them anyway. I used to trade my apple slices for an extra slice of pizza with this kid named Brian. He was super into skateboarding, always wore those chunky DC shoes with the fat laces... 

Wait, what were we talking about? Days without the heavy feeling? Yeah, no, I don't think I've had a single day where it's just gone. It's pretty much constant.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.21, F43.22, F43.23\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'm here because... ever since my daughter left for basic training, I just feel like I come home to a dead house. She's down at Fort Jackson right now. You know, people call it \"Relaxin' Jackson\" but she says the humidity is just awful down there. I went to Myrtle Beach once when I was a teenager, and the heat in South Carolina is honestly no joke, it just ruins your hair. Not that I worry about my hair much at the shop with the helmet on, but... \n\nAnyway, I just can’t keep myself together lately. I'm literally sitting there crying in my truck before my shifts even start. It's an '08 F-150, and the transmission is starting to slip on it, which is a whole other headache I don't want to deal with right now. My buddy Dave said he'd look at it, but he's always busy. \n\nBut yeah, at work, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I’ve done for years. It's bad. And I keep snapping at the younger guys on the crew. Though, to be fair, these kids today are always looking at their phones, right? One of them almost dropped a sheet of 16-gauge steel on his foot last week because he was watching a video. But I know it's my temper that's the problem right now. I'm just so off. I've even stopped going out on the water, and I haven't worked on my metal sculptures at home at all. I used to love making things out of scrap, but I just don’t see the point anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since your daughter left for basic training?\n\nPatient: It's been about a month since she left. Yeah, right around four weeks now that I've been just... struggling like this. I dropped her off at the recruiter's office on a Tuesday, I remember because it was raining so hard we almost had to pull over on the interstate. The wipers on that F-150 just couldn't keep up. I've tried changing the blades, you know, bought those expensive silicone ones from AutoZone that are supposed to last a year, but they still streak on the driver's side. It's so annoying when you're trying to see the road glare at night. But yeah, ever since that day she left, I've just been a mess. I haven't even gone to the grocery store for real food, just been living off those frozen dinners. The Salisbury steak ones aren't terrible if you put enough hot sauce on them, but it's not the same as cooking. I used to make these great pork chops... but yeah, it's been about a month.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're no longer enjoying things that you used to enjoy, or is it just that you don't have the energy to do them?\n\nPatient: I don't know, it's... I guess it's a little bit of both? Like I said, I haven't been working on my sculptures at all. I used to go out to the garage every weekend and just weld scrap pieces together. Last month I was working on this heron made out of old spark plugs and rebar. It was actually turning out really good. I get the spark plugs from my neighbor, Jim. He runs a small engine repair business out of his shed. You wouldn't believe how many lawnmowers people just throw away because they don't want to clean a carburetor. Jim fixes them up and sells them on Marketplace. He made like eight hundred bucks last spring just on riding mowers. He's been trying to get me to buy one, but my yard isn't even that big, you know? Just a little patch of grass in the back that my dog mostly tears up anyway. He's a terrier mix, crazy amount of energy. But yeah, I just... I go out to the garage, look at the welding torch, and I just feel so tired. I don't even want to turn it on. I just end up going back inside and sitting on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your daughter leaving for basic training?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm proud of her. She's doing something important, you know? The military is a solid career path. My uncle was in the Navy, actually. He was stationed out in San Diego back in the eighties. He always talked about how beautiful the weather was out there, like seventy degrees and sunny every single day. I've always wanted to go to California, maybe see the Redwoods or drive up the Pacific Coast Highway. I saw this documentary once about how those trees can live for thousands of years, it's just wild to think about. But anyway, no, I don't feel guilty. I just miss her so much it physically hurts. It's just me and the dog now, and he's not exactly great at conversation, unless you count barking at the mailman every morning at 10 AM sharp. The new mail carrier is this young guy who always wears shorts, even when it's freezing out. I don't know how his legs don't turn purple. But yeah, no guilt, just... just this heavy feeling all the time.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your job as a welder because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... it has been bad at the shop. My supervisor ended up taking me off this really complex welding job we were doing because he said I wasn't coordinating safely enough. Which, I mean, safety is a huge deal for us. We have this big whiteboard in the breakroom tracking our days without an incident. It was up to like two hundred days until Dave dropped a heavy piece of ductwork on his foot last month. He wasn't wearing his steel-toed boots, which is ridiculous because there's a Red Wing store right down the street that gives us a union discount. I always buy the lace-up ones, even though they take forever to break in. I usually have to wear two pairs of thick wool socks for the first week just to stop the blisters from forming on my heels. My grandma used to knit these amazing wool socks, she'd make them in all these crazy neon colors so you couldn't lose them in the laundry. I still have a pair that's bright orange, though they're getting a little threadbare at the toes now. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, they took me off that job. And it's not just at work, either. I had this deadline for a sculpture submission for a local gallery, and I just... I just let it pass. I didn't even finish the piece. The gallery is right next to this amazing Thai place, actually. They make the best drunken noodles, though the last time I went, they made it so spicy I was practically sweating through my shirt. The guy who owns it is always trying to give me free spring rolls when I pick up my takeout. But yeah... I just couldn't bring myself to finish the sculpture, and I just can't seem to focus on the shop floor right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you no longer interested in doing things you used to enjoy, like welding and making sculptures?\n\nPatient: I mean, I kind of talked about that a minute ago, right? I don't really know if it's that I've completely lost interest forever, it's just... I look at the tools and I just feel nothing. Usually, I'd at least go out there and turn on the radio. I have this old boombox in the garage that actually still plays cassette tapes. I used to make mixtapes all the time back in high school. You know, sitting by the stereo for hours just waiting for a specific song to come on the radio so you could hit record at the exact right second? I had this one tape that was all Aerosmith and Def Leppard. My older brother actually stole it from me when he bought his first car, this completely rusted-out Chevy Cavalier. The muffler on that thing was so bad it sounded like a jet engine taking off in our driveway. The neighbors actually called the cops on him once because he came home at two in the morning. The cop who showed up was actually a guy he played varsity football with, which is funny, small world, right? But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the sculptures and stuff. Yeah, I just... I don't know. I just don't really care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty taking care of your basic needs, like eating or sleeping, because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I think I'm doing okay with that stuff. I mean, I eat when I'm hungry. I've been making a lot of sandwiches lately. It's just easy, you know? Just throw some turkey and cheese on bread. I actually found this really good sourdough at the bakery downtown. The one that used to be a shoe repair place? They kept the old neon sign in the window, shaped like a giant boot. I always thought that was a neat touch. The guy who runs the bakery, his name is Paul, he's got this massive handlebar mustache. It's perfectly curled at the ends, like an old-timey villain. He said he uses some special wax he orders from Germany. My grandfather had a mustache like that, but he never waxed it. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how hard he scrubbed them with that orange pumice soap. I love the smell of that soap, it always reminds me of his garage. He had this old calendar on the wall from 1994 with vintage cars on it that he just refused to take down. But yeah, eating and sleeping, I'm getting by fine. I sleep okay, mostly. Just, you know, tired a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... I don't really know if sad is the right word. It's more just like this heavy feeling, I guess? Like I'm just dragging. I felt kind of like this when my dog died a few years ago. He was a golden retriever mix named Buster. We got him from the shelter when he was already like five years old, so he was already pretty mellow. He used to just lay under the kitchen table while I cooked. I make a really good chili, by the way. It's my dad's recipe, he always put a little bit of dark chocolate in it to cut the acidity of the tomatoes. Sounds weird, but it totally works. He won a chili cook-off at his church with it back in, like, 2012. They gave him this incredibly tacky trophy shaped like a giant chili pepper wearing a sombrero. He kept it on the mantelpiece right next to my high school graduation photo, which I always hated because I had these awful braces with rubber bands that I chose in neon green because I thought I was being edgy. I spent so much time at the orthodontist's office, Dr. Miller. He had this massive fish tank in the waiting room with these huge angelfish... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Sadness? Yeah, I don't know, I just... I'm just not feeling like myself, but I wouldn't say I'm crying all day or anything. Just heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this heavy feeling all the time, or are there moments when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to it like that. I don't really track it, you know? I was actually trying to figure that out yesterday when I was at the shop. We had this massive shipment of aluminum diamond plate come in. Usually, I don't mind working with that stuff, it's got a good texture to it. But my coworker, Dave, he always wears these ridiculous heavy-duty leather gloves with the fingers cut off when he unloads it. He swears it gives him better dexterity, but I keep telling him he's gonna slice a finger off on a raw edge one of these days. \n\nHe actually did cut himself pretty bad last year, but it wasn't even on the metal. My uncle used to make his own venison jerky in this giant dehydrator he kept down in his basement. The whole house would smell like hickory smoke and black pepper for days. He was a huge hunter, had all these taxidermy deer heads mounted on the wood paneling in his den. They used to completely freak my younger cousins out when we'd go over there for Thanksgiving... \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Oh, if the heavy feeling lifts. Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you. I just haven't really noticed it going away.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel this heavy feeling at all?\n\nPatient: Days where I don't feel it at all? Lost interest? I don't know, maybe both? It's just... since my daughter left for basic training, I feel like I come home to a dead house. It's so quiet. She used to play her music so loud, this awful screaming metal stuff that drove me crazy, but now I'd give anything to hear it. She had this beat-up old Honda Civic that she covered in bumper stickers, mostly bands I'd never heard of and some weird political ones. We spent a whole weekend replacing the alternator on that thing last summer. It was sweltering, like 95 degrees with 100% humidity, and we were both covered in grease and sweat, just cursing at this one stubborn bolt that wouldn't budge. We ended up having to use a breaker bar and a pipe to get enough leverage. Afterward, we went and got these massive milkshakes from that old diner on Route 9. They make them with real ice cream, not that soft-serve garbage. I got a black and white, and she got strawberry. She always loved strawberry anything since she was a little kid. She had this Strawberry Shortcake doll that smelled like fake fruit, and she dragged it everywhere until the yarn hair was completely matted...

Sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, things I enjoy. Yeah, I just... I can't keep myself together. I'm crying in my truck before shifts, which is just pathetic. I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years, just completely losing focus. And I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop for no reason. I haven't even been out on the water or worked on my metal sculpture in weeks. I just don't see the point anymore.

Um... no, I don't think so. It's just kind of always there, dragging me down. Like I wake up and it's already sitting on my chest. I actually woke up super early this morning, like 4:00 AM, because the garbage truck was making this horrendous grinding noise outside my apartment. They usually don't come until Thursday, but I guess because of the holiday on Monday their whole schedule is messed up. I hate when schedules get messed up. It reminds me of when I was in high school and they changed the bell schedule halfway through the year to add this weird advisory period. I got lost on the way to my locker like three times the first week. My locker was right next to the cafeteria, so it always smelled like tater tots and bleach. They served these square pizzas on Fridays that were basically cardboard with cheese, but everybody loved them anyway. I used to trade my apple slices for an extra slice of pizza with this kid named Brian. He was super into skateboarding, always wore those chunky DC shoes with the fat laces... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Days without the heavy feeling? Yeah, no, I don't think I've had a single day where it's just gone. It's pretty much constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost interest in the things you used to enjoy, or is it just that you don't feel like doing them right now?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe both? It's just... since my daughter left for basic training, I feel like I come home to a dead house. It's so quiet. She used to play her music so loud, this awful screaming metal stuff that drove me crazy, but now I'd give anything to hear it. She had this beat-up old Honda Civic that she covered in bumper stickers, mostly bands I'd never heard of and some weird political ones. We spent a whole weekend replacing the alternator on that thing last summer. It was sweltering, like 95 degrees with 100% humidity, and we were both covered in grease and sweat, just cursing at this one stubborn bolt that wouldn't budge. We ended up having to use a breaker bar and a pipe to get enough leverage. Afterward, we went and got these massive milkshakes from that old diner on Route 9. They make them with real ice cream, not that soft-serve garbage. I got a black and white, and she got strawberry. She always loved strawberry anything since she was a little kid. She had this Strawberry Shortcake doll that smelled like fake fruit, and she dragged it everywhere until the yarn hair was completely matted...\n\nSorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, things I enjoy. Yeah, I just... I can't keep myself together. I'm crying in my truck before shifts, which is just pathetic. I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years, just completely losing focus. And I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop for no reason. I haven't even been out on the water or worked on my metal sculpture in weeks. I just don't see the point anymore.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you started feeling this way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 38 / sheet_metal_worker / high_school\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Changes with friends or family? I don't really know. I guess I haven't been seeing anyone enough to even notice. My sister did call me on Sunday, though. She was all worked up because her neighbor's dog keeps digging under her fence. It's this huge Golden Retriever, really goofy looking thing, but it keeps tearing up her hostas. She spent like three hundred dollars on landscaping this spring. She got all these decorative river rocks and laid down that black weed barrier fabric. I told her that fabric is a complete scam, the weeds just grow right on top of it anyway. You're better off just using a thick layer of cedar mulch. Cedar smells so much better, too. It reminds me of this cedar chest my grandmother had at the foot of her bed. She kept all these old handmade quilts in there, the ones made out of scraps from my grandpa's old work shirts. He was a diesel mechanic, always wore these heavy canvas shirts that took forever to dry on the clothesline. They had this old rotary washing machine out on the back porch... 

Wait, what did you ask me? Changes with people? Yeah, I don't know. I just haven't been around anyone to really tell you.

I've always wanted to go to California, maybe see the Redwoods or drive up the Pacific Coast Highway. He wasn't wearing his steel-toed boots, which is ridiculous because there's a Red Wing store right down the street that gives us a union discount. I always buy the lace-up ones, even though they take forever to break in. I usually have to wear two pairs of thick wool socks for the first week just to stop the blisters from forming on my heels. My grandma used to knit these amazing wool socks, she'd make them in all these crazy neon colors so you couldn't lose them in the laundry. I still have a pair that's bright orange, though they're getting a little threadbare at the toes now. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, they took me off that job. The gallery is right next to this amazing Thai place, actually. They make the best drunken noodles, though the last time I went, they made it so spicy I was practically sweating through my shirt. He said he uses some special wax he orders from Germany. My grandfather had a mustache like that, but he never waxed it. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how hard he scrubbed them with that orange pumice soap. I love the smell of that soap, it always reminds me of his garage. He had this old calendar on the wall from 1994 with vintage cars on it that he just refused to take down. But yeah, eating and sleeping, I'm getting by fine. I sleep okay, mostly. He won a chili cook-off at his church with it back in, like, 2012. They gave him this incredibly tacky trophy shaped like a giant chili pepper wearing a sombrero. You know, sitting by the stereo for hours just waiting for a specific song to come on the radio so you could hit record at the exact right second? I had this one tape that was all Aerosmith and Def Leppard. My older brother actually stole it from me when he bought his first car, this completely rusted-out Chevy Cavalier. The muffler on that thing was so bad it sounded like a jet engine taking off in our driveway. The neighbors actually called the cops on him once because he came home at two in the morning. The cop who showed up was actually a guy he played varsity football with, which is funny, small world, right? But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the sculptures and stuff. Yeah, I just... I don't know. I just don't really care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty taking care of your basic needs, like eating or sleeping, because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I think I'm doing okay with that stuff. I mean, I eat when I'm hungry. I've been making a lot of sandwiches lately. It's just easy, you know? Just throw some turkey and cheese on bread. I actually found this really good sourdough at the bakery downtown. The one that used to be a shoe repair place? They kept the old neon sign in the window, shaped like a giant boot. I always thought that was a neat touch. The guy who runs the bakery, his name is Paul, he's got this massive handlebar mustache. It's perfectly curled at the ends, like an old-timey villain. He said he uses some special wax he orders from Germany. My grandfather had a mustache like that, but he never waxed it. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how hard he scrubbed them with that orange pumice soap. I love the smell of that soap, it always reminds me of his garage. He had this old calendar on the wall from 1994 with vintage cars on it that he just refused to take down. But yeah, eating and sleeping, I'm getting by fine. I sleep okay, mostly. Just, you know, tired a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... I don't really know if sad is the right word. It's more just like this heavy feeling, I guess? Like I'm just dragging. I felt kind of like this when my dog died a few years ago. He was a golden retriever mix named Buster. We got him from the shelter when he was already like five years old, so he was already pretty mellow. He used to just lay under the kitchen table while I cooked. I make a really good chili, by the way. It's my dad's recipe, he always put a little bit of dark chocolate in it to cut the acidity of the tomatoes. Sounds weird, but it totally works. He won a chili cook-off at his church with it back in, like, 2012. They gave him this incredibly tacky trophy shaped like a giant chili pepper wearing a sombrero. He kept it on the mantelpiece right next to my high school graduation photo, which I always hated because I had these awful braces with rubber bands that I chose in neon green because I thought I was being edgy. I spent so much time at the orthodontist's office, Dr. Miller. He had this massive fish tank in the waiting room with these huge angelfish... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Sadness? Yeah, I don't know, I just... I'm just not feeling like myself, but I wouldn't say I'm crying all day or anything. Just heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this heavy feeling all the time, or are there moments when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to it like that. I don't really track it, you know? I was actually trying to figure that out yesterday when I was at the shop. We had this massive shipment of aluminum diamond plate come in. Usually, I don't mind working with that stuff, it's got a good texture to it. But my coworker, Dave, he always wears these ridiculous heavy-duty leather gloves with the fingers cut off when he unloads it. He swears it gives him better dexterity, but I keep telling him he's gonna slice a finger off on a raw edge one of these days. \n\nHe actually did cut himself pretty bad last year, but it wasn't even on the metal. He was trying to open one of those hard plastic clamshell packages of beef jerky with a box cutter. You know the packages I'm talking about, right? The ones that are completely impossible to open without destroying your hands. It was teriyaki flavor, too, which I've always thought is way too sweet. I definitely prefer the peppered kind. My uncle used to make his own venison jerky in this giant dehydrator he kept down in his basement. The whole house would smell like hickory smoke and black pepper for days. He was a huge hunter, had all these taxidermy deer heads mounted on the wood paneling in his den. They used to completely freak my younger cousins out when we'd go over there for Thanksgiving... \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Oh, if the heavy feeling lifts. Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you. I just haven't really noticed it going away.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel this heavy feeling at all?\n\nPatient: Days where I don't feel it at all? Um... no, I don't think so. It's just kind of always there, dragging me down. Like I wake up and it's already sitting on my chest. I actually woke up super early this morning, like 4:00 AM, because the garbage truck was making this horrendous grinding noise outside my apartment. They usually don't come until Thursday, but I guess because of the holiday on Monday their whole schedule is messed up. I hate when schedules get messed up. It reminds me of when I was in high school and they changed the bell schedule halfway through the year to add this weird advisory period. I got lost on the way to my locker like three times the first week. My locker was right next to the cafeteria, so it always smelled like tater tots and bleach. They served these square pizzas on Fridays that were basically cardboard with cheese, but everybody loved them anyway. I used to trade my apple slices for an extra slice of pizza with this kid named Brian. He was super into skateboarding, always wore those chunky DC shoes with the fat laces... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Days without the heavy feeling? Yeah, no, I don't think I've had a single day where it's just gone. It's pretty much constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost interest in the things you used to enjoy, or is it just that you don't feel like doing them right now?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe both? It's just... since my daughter left for basic training, I feel like I come home to a dead house. It's so quiet. She used to play her music so loud, this awful screaming metal stuff that drove me crazy, but now I'd give anything to hear it. She had this beat-up old Honda Civic that she covered in bumper stickers, mostly bands I'd never heard of and some weird political ones. We spent a whole weekend replacing the alternator on that thing last summer. It was sweltering, like 95 degrees with 100% humidity, and we were both covered in grease and sweat, just cursing at this one stubborn bolt that wouldn't budge. We ended up having to use a breaker bar and a pipe to get enough leverage. Afterward, we went and got these massive milkshakes from that old diner on Route 9. They make them with real ice cream, not that soft-serve garbage. I got a black and white, and she got strawberry. She always loved strawberry anything since she was a little kid. She had this Strawberry Shortcake doll that smelled like fake fruit, and she dragged it everywhere until the yarn hair was completely matted...\n\nSorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, things I enjoy. Yeah, I just... I can't keep myself together. I'm crying in my truck before shifts, which is just pathetic. I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years, just completely losing focus. And I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop for no reason. I haven't even been out on the water or worked on my metal sculpture in weeks. I just don't see the point anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Changes with friends or family? I don't really know. I guess I haven't been seeing anyone enough to even notice. My sister did call me on Sunday, though. She was all worked up because her neighbor's dog keeps digging under her fence. It's this huge Golden Retriever, really goofy looking thing, but it keeps tearing up her hostas. She spent like three hundred dollars on landscaping this spring. She got all these decorative river rocks and laid down that black weed barrier fabric. I told her that fabric is a complete scam, the weeds just grow right on top of it anyway. You're better off just using a thick layer of cedar mulch. Cedar smells so much better, too. It reminds me of this cedar chest my grandmother had at the foot of her bed. She kept all these old handmade quilts in there, the ones made out of scraps from my grandpa's old work shirts. He was a diesel mechanic, always wore these heavy canvas shirts that took forever to dry on the clothesline. They had this old rotary washing machine out on the back porch... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Changes with people? Yeah, I don't know. I just haven't been around anyone to really tell you.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Weight changes? Um... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really been paying attention to the scale or anything. I did have to buy a new belt a few weeks ago, though. The buckle on my old one just completely snapped off right when I was getting into my truck. It was this heavy leather belt I got at a craft fair up in the mountains a few years back. The guy who made it had this whole setup with these intricate tooling stamps. He was showing me how he gets the leather wet first so it takes the impression better. He had this massive beard, like ZZ Top level, and he was wearing these really cool vintage suspenders. I always wanted to try leatherworking, but the tools are so expensive. It's kind of like welding, you know? The initial investment for a decent TIG welder and all the argon tanks and safety gear is just astronomical. And don't even get me started on the cost of tungsten electrodes right now. Supply chain issues are just ruining everything. I went to the hardware store last week to get some basic hex nuts and they were completely out of the size I needed. The guy working there said they've been waiting on a shipment for a month. He looked really tired, honestly. He was drinking out of one of those giant insulated mugs, the ones that hold like a gallon of coffee...

Sorry, what were we talking about? Weight? Yeah, I'm not really sure. I haven't noticed anything major, just haven't been keeping track.

But I know it's my temper that's the problem right now. I'm just so off. I've even stopped going out on the water, and I haven't worked on my metal sculptures at home at all. I used to love making things out of scrap, but I just don’t see the point anymore.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way since your daughter left for basic training?\n\nPatient: It's been about a month since she left. Yeah, right around four weeks now that I've been just... struggling like this.
