Last month I was working on this heron made out of old spark plugs and rebar. It was actually turning out really good. I get the spark plugs from my neighbor, Jim. He runs a small engine repair business out of his shed. You wouldn't believe how many lawnmowers people just throw away because they don't want to clean a carburetor. Jim fixes them up and sells them on Marketplace. He made like eight hundred bucks last spring just on riding mowers. He's been trying to get me to buy one, but my yard isn't even that big, you know? Just a little patch of grass in the back that my dog mostly tears up anyway. He's a terrier mix, crazy amount of energy. But yeah, I just... I go out to the garage, look at the welding torch, and I just feel so tired. I don't even want to turn it on. I just end up going back inside and sitting on the couch.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty about anything related to your daughter leaving for basic training?\n\nPatient: Guilty? No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm proud of her. She's doing something important, you know? The military is a solid career path. My uncle was in the Navy, actually. He was stationed out in San Diego back in the eighties. He always talked about how beautiful the weather was out there, like seventy degrees and sunny every single day. I've always wanted to go to California, maybe see the Redwoods or drive up the Pacific Coast Highway. I saw this documentary once about how those trees can live for thousands of years, it's just wild to think about. But anyway, no, I don't feel guilty. I just miss her so much it physically hurts. It's just me and the dog now, and he's not exactly great at conversation, unless you count barking at the mailman every morning at 10 AM sharp. The new mail carrier is this young guy who always wears shorts, even when it's freezing out. I don't know how his legs don't turn purple. But yeah, no guilt, just... just this heavy feeling all the time.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your job as a welder because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually... it has been bad at the shop. My supervisor ended up taking me off this really complex welding job we were doing because he said I wasn't coordinating safely enough. Which, I mean, safety is a huge deal for us. We have this big whiteboard in the breakroom tracking our days without an incident. It was up to like two hundred days until Dave dropped a heavy piece of ductwork on his foot last month. He wasn't wearing his steel-toed boots, which is ridiculous because there's a Red Wing store right down the street that gives us a union discount. I always buy the lace-up ones, even though they take forever to break in. I usually have to wear two pairs of thick wool socks for the first week just to stop the blisters from forming on my heels. My grandma used to knit these amazing wool socks, she'd make them in all these crazy neon colors so you couldn't lose them in the laundry. I still have a pair that's bright orange, though they're getting a little threadbare at the toes now. \n\nBut, um, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, they took me off that job. And it's not just at work, either. I had this deadline for a sculpture submission for a local gallery, and I just... I just let it pass. I didn't even finish the piece. The gallery is right next to this amazing Thai place, actually. They make the best drunken noodles, though the last time I went, they made it so spicy I was practically sweating through my shirt. The guy who owns it is always trying to give me free spring rolls when I pick up my takeout. But yeah... I just couldn't bring myself to finish the sculpture, and I just can't seem to focus on the shop floor right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you no longer interested in doing things you used to enjoy, like welding and making sculptures?\n\nPatient: I mean, I kind of talked about that a minute ago, right? I don't really know if it's that I've completely lost interest forever, it's just... I look at the tools and I just feel nothing. Usually, I'd at least go out there and turn on the radio. I have this old boombox in the garage that actually still plays cassette tapes. I used to make mixtapes all the time back in high school. You know, sitting by the stereo for hours just waiting for a specific song to come on the radio so you could hit record at the exact right second? I had this one tape that was all Aerosmith and Def Leppard. My older brother actually stole it from me when he bought his first car, this completely rusted-out Chevy Cavalier. The muffler on that thing was so bad it sounded like a jet engine taking off in our driveway. The neighbors actually called the cops on him once because he came home at two in the morning. The cop who showed up was actually a guy he played varsity football with, which is funny, small world, right? But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, the sculptures and stuff. Yeah, I just... I don't know. I just don't really care about doing any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you having difficulty taking care of your basic needs, like eating or sleeping, because of how you're feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I think I'm doing okay with that stuff. I mean, I eat when I'm hungry. I've been making a lot of sandwiches lately. It's just easy, you know? Just throw some turkey and cheese on bread. I actually found this really good sourdough at the bakery downtown. The one that used to be a shoe repair place? They kept the old neon sign in the window, shaped like a giant boot. I always thought that was a neat touch. The guy who runs the bakery, his name is Paul, he's got this massive handlebar mustache. It's perfectly curled at the ends, like an old-timey villain. He said he uses some special wax he orders from Germany. My grandfather had a mustache like that, but he never waxed it. He was a mechanic, always had grease under his fingernails no matter how hard he scrubbed them with that orange pumice soap. I love the smell of that soap, it always reminds me of his garage. He had this old calendar on the wall from 1994 with vintage cars on it that he just refused to take down. But yeah, eating and sleeping, I'm getting by fine. I sleep okay, mostly. Just, you know, tired a lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Sad? Um... I don't really know if sad is the right word. It's more just like this heavy feeling, I guess? Like I'm just dragging. I felt kind of like this when my dog died a few years ago. He was a golden retriever mix named Buster. We got him from the shelter when he was already like five years old, so he was already pretty mellow. He used to just lay under the kitchen table while I cooked. I make a really good chili, by the way. It's my dad's recipe, he always put a little bit of dark chocolate in it to cut the acidity of the tomatoes. Sounds weird, but it totally works. He won a chili cook-off at his church with it back in, like, 2012. They gave him this incredibly tacky trophy shaped like a giant chili pepper wearing a sombrero. He kept it on the mantelpiece right next to my high school graduation photo, which I always hated because I had these awful braces with rubber bands that I chose in neon green because I thought I was being edgy. I spent so much time at the orthodontist's office, Dr. Miller. He had this massive fish tank in the waiting room with these huge angelfish... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Sadness? Yeah, I don't know, I just... I'm just not feeling like myself, but I wouldn't say I'm crying all day or anything. Just heavy.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this heavy feeling all the time, or are there moments when it lifts a bit?\n\nPatient: I mean, I haven't really been paying attention to it like that. I don't really track it, you know? I was actually trying to figure that out yesterday when I was at the shop. We had this massive shipment of aluminum diamond plate come in. Usually, I don't mind working with that stuff, it's got a good texture to it. But my coworker, Dave, he always wears these ridiculous heavy-duty leather gloves with the fingers cut off when he unloads it. He swears it gives him better dexterity, but I keep telling him he's gonna slice a finger off on a raw edge one of these days. \n\nHe actually did cut himself pretty bad last year, but it wasn't even on the metal. He was trying to open one of those hard plastic clamshell packages of beef jerky with a box cutter. You know the packages I'm talking about, right? The ones that are completely impossible to open without destroying your hands. It was teriyaki flavor, too, which I've always thought is way too sweet. I definitely prefer the peppered kind. My uncle used to make his own venison jerky in this giant dehydrator he kept down in his basement. The whole house would smell like hickory smoke and black pepper for days. He was a huge hunter, had all these taxidermy deer heads mounted on the wood paneling in his den. They used to completely freak my younger cousins out when we'd go over there for Thanksgiving... \n\nSorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? What was the question? Oh, if the heavy feeling lifts. Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you. I just haven't really noticed it going away.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have days where you don't feel this heavy feeling at all?\n\nPatient: Days where I don't feel it at all? Um... no, I don't think so. It's just kind of always there, dragging me down. Like I wake up and it's already sitting on my chest. I actually woke up super early this morning, like 4:00 AM, because the garbage truck was making this horrendous grinding noise outside my apartment. They usually don't come until Thursday, but I guess because of the holiday on Monday their whole schedule is messed up. I hate when schedules get messed up. It reminds me of when I was in high school and they changed the bell schedule halfway through the year to add this weird advisory period. I got lost on the way to my locker like three times the first week. My locker was right next to the cafeteria, so it always smelled like tater tots and bleach. They served these square pizzas on Fridays that were basically cardboard with cheese, but everybody loved them anyway. I used to trade my apple slices for an extra slice of pizza with this kid named Brian. He was super into skateboarding, always wore those chunky DC shoes with the fat laces... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Days without the heavy feeling? Yeah, no, I don't think I've had a single day where it's just gone. It's pretty much constant.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you've lost interest in the things you used to enjoy, or is it just that you don't feel like doing them right now?\n\nPatient: Lost interest? I don't know, maybe both? It's just... since my daughter left for basic training, I feel like I come home to a dead house. It's so quiet. She used to play her music so loud, this awful screaming metal stuff that drove me crazy, but now I'd give anything to hear it. She had this beat-up old Honda Civic that she covered in bumper stickers, mostly bands I'd never heard of and some weird political ones. We spent a whole weekend replacing the alternator on that thing last summer. It was sweltering, like 95 degrees with 100% humidity, and we were both covered in grease and sweat, just cursing at this one stubborn bolt that wouldn't budge. We ended up having to use a breaker bar and a pipe to get enough leverage. Afterward, we went and got these massive milkshakes from that old diner on Route 9. They make them with real ice cream, not that soft-serve garbage. I got a black and white, and she got strawberry. She always loved strawberry anything since she was a little kid. She had this Strawberry Shortcake doll that smelled like fake fruit, and she dragged it everywhere until the yarn hair was completely matted...\n\nSorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, things I enjoy. Yeah, I just... I can't keep myself together. I'm crying in my truck before shifts, which is just pathetic. I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years, just completely losing focus. And I'm snapping at the younger guys at the shop for no reason. I haven't even been out on the water or worked on my metal sculpture in weeks. I just don't see the point anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Changes with friends or family? I don't really know. I guess I haven't been seeing anyone enough to even notice. My sister did call me on Sunday, though. She was all worked up because her neighbor's dog keeps digging under her fence. It's this huge Golden Retriever, really goofy looking thing, but it keeps tearing up her hostas. She spent like three hundred dollars on landscaping this spring. She got all these decorative river rocks and laid down that black weed barrier fabric. I told her that fabric is a complete scam, the weeds just grow right on top of it anyway. You're better off just using a thick layer of cedar mulch. Cedar smells so much better, too. It reminds me of this cedar chest my grandmother had at the foot of her bed. She kept all these old handmade quilts in there, the ones made out of scraps from my grandpa's old work shirts. He was a diesel mechanic, always wore these heavy canvas shirts that took forever to dry on the clothesline. They had this old rotary washing machine out on the back porch... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Changes with people? Yeah, I don't know. I just haven't been around anyone to really tell you.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since your daughter left for basic training?\n\nPatient: Weight changes? Um... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really been paying attention to the scale or anything. I did have to buy a new belt a few weeks ago, though. The buckle on my old one just completely snapped off right when I was getting into my truck. It was this heavy leather belt I got at a craft fair up in the mountains a few years back. The guy who made it had this whole setup with these intricate tooling stamps. He was showing me how he gets the leather wet first so it takes the impression better. He had this massive beard, like ZZ Top level, and he was wearing these really cool vintage suspenders. I always wanted to try leatherworking, but the tools are so expensive. It's kind of like welding, you know? The initial investment for a decent TIG welder and all the argon tanks and safety gear is just astronomical. And don't even get me started on the cost of tungsten electrodes right now. Supply chain issues are just ruining everything. I went to the hardware store last week to get some basic hex nuts and they were completely out of the size I needed. The guy working there said they've been waiting on a shipment for a month. He looked really tired, honestly. He was drinking out of one of those giant insulated mugs, the ones that hold like a gallon of coffee...\n\nSorry, what were we talking about? Weight? Yeah, I'm not really sure. I haven't noticed anything major, just haven't been keeping track.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Activities I still enjoy? I mean... not really. I've just been sitting out on my porch a lot, honestly, not even doing anything. Just staring at the street. There's this guy who drives past every evening in a restored '69 Chevy C10. Beautiful truck, painted this deep metallic cherry red. You can tell whoever did the bodywork really took their time with the block sanding, because the reflection on the quarter panels is like glass. I used to mess around with auto body repair before I got my union card for sheet metal. Working with Bondo is such a pain, though. The dust gets absolutely everywhere. You'll be blowing it out of your nose for days, and it gets in your coffee. I used to get my coffee from this little drive-thru shack near the old shop. The woman who owned it kept a pet parrot in there, like a full-size macaw just sitting on a perch next to the espresso machine. It would mimic the sound of the milk steamer perfectly, just this loud hissing noise. It was hilarious, but honestly, it had to be a massive health code violation. The health inspector in our county is this older guy, real hard-ass, he actually shut down the deli on Main Street because their walk-in cooler was running like, two degrees too warm. Two degrees! And they had just prepped all their potato salad for the Memorial Day parade...\n\nI'm sorry, what was the question? Activities? Yeah, no. I don't really have anything I want to do. I'm not really sure there's anything I enjoy right now. Just... nothing sounds good anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about death or wanting to harm yourself since your daughter left for basic training?\n\nPatient: Thoughts about death? Harming myself? No, no, nothing like that. I mean, I've just been sitting around feeling empty, not wanting to die or anything. I don't think I could ever do that, honestly. I don't even like the sight of blood. Last time I had to get blood drawn for my physical, the phlebotomist couldn't find a vein. She kept digging around with the needle in my left arm, which is already sensitive because I got a bad sunburn on it last summer. I fell asleep in a lawn chair at my cousin's barbecue. He makes the best ribs, slow-cooks them over hickory wood for like eight hours. He built his own smoker out of an old oil drum. It's actually a pretty genius setup, he welded on these custom hinges and installed a temperature gauge right on the lid. The last job site I was on, before I took some time off, we were installing ductwork in this massive commercial freezer facility. It was freezing in there, literally. You had to wear thermal gloves just to handle the sheet metal or your skin would stick to it. I hate wearing those bulky gloves, you lose all your dexterity. It's impossible to get a good grip on the tin snips...\n\nWait, sorry. Did you ask about hurting myself? Yeah, no. I haven't had any thoughts like that. Just... feeling really down and not wanting to do anything. But no thoughts of death.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find pleasure in only slightly or not at all?\n\nPatient: Activities I used to enjoy? Yeah, I mean... I used to love going out on the water. I have this little aluminum john boat I bought off a guy on Craigslist a few years ago. It needed a ton of work, the transom was completely rotted out. I had to replace the wood and seal it all up with marine-grade epoxy. That epoxy smells awful, by the way. It’s this two-part mixture, and if you don’t get the ratio exactly right, it never cures properly. It just stays tacky forever. I learned that the hard way when I tried to fix a fiberglass canoe for my brother. He ended up having to sand the whole thing down and start over. Sanding fiberglass is the absolute worst. The dust gets in your pores and makes you itch for days, even if you wear a Tyvek suit. Those suits are so hot, too. You wear one in the summer and you’re sweating buckets within five minutes. It’s like wearing a trash bag. I remember wearing one when we had to do some ductwork in a crawlspace under this old Victorian house. The place was full of spider webs and old newspapers from like, the 1940s. I actually found an old glass Coca-Cola bottle down there, the kind with the thick green glass. People collect those, you know. There's a whole market for vintage bottles...\n\nSorry, I'm rambling again. What did you ask? Things I used to enjoy? Yeah, the boat. And I used to do some metal sculpture in the garage, just welding scrap pieces together. But since my daughter left for basic training... I just don't see the point anymore. I haven't touched the boat or the welder. I come home to a dead house and I just can't keep myself together. I'm sitting in my truck crying before my shifts start. And at work, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years, just completely losing my focus. I've even been snapping at the younger guys on the crew, which isn't like me. I just... I don't care about the stuff I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Activities I still enjoy? I think you asked me that already, actually. But honestly, no. There's just nothing. I don't get pleasure out of anything right now. It's just... flat. Like a sheet of 24-gauge galvanized steel before it goes through the brake press. That machine is something else, by the way. It exerts tons of pressure, literally tons, just to bend the metal. You have to be so careful with your fingers. A guy I used to work with, Dave, he lost the tip of his index finger to one of those presses back in the nineties. He always joked about it, though. Said it made it easier to pick his nose. Dave was a character. He used to bring these massive, overstuffed meatball subs for lunch every single Friday. Got them from this little Italian place run by this old couple. The husband made the meatballs from scratch every morning at 4 AM. You could smell the garlic from halfway down the block. I tried making meatballs once, but I used too much breadcrumb and they turned out like hockey pucks. My dog wouldn't even eat them. He’s a golden retriever mix, eats literally everything, including a tube of chapstick once. I had to call poison control, and they just laughed and said he'd have very moisturized poops for a couple days... \n\nAnyway, sorry. No, I don't really find pleasure in anything at all right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy your old activities if you had the energy to do them?\n\nPatient: If I had the energy? I don't know. I mean, maybe? It's hard to even picture it right now. I just feel so drained all the time. Like when you run a battery down to absolutely zero, you know? Like those 18-volt lithium-ion batteries we use for the impact drivers at work. If you leave them out in the cold truck overnight, they just completely die on you. The cold just saps the life right out of them. My old foreman, he used to make us bring all the batteries into the job trailer at the end of the shift during the winter. He had this whole charging station set up, custom built it himself out of some scrap plywood and two-by-fours. He was a real stickler for organization. Every tool had its place, outlined in black marker on the pegboard. If you put a pair of Vice-Grips back in the wrong spot, you'd hear about it for a week. He was tough, but he knew his stuff. He retired a couple years ago, moved down to Florida. Bought a place near Tampa, I think. Or maybe it was Clearwater? Somewhere near the Gulf. I went down to the Gulf once, when I was in my twenties. The water is so warm there, like bathwater. Nothing like the ocean up here. Up here, you go in up to your knees and you can't feel your toes after five minutes...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Energy. Yeah, I don't know. I just... I can't imagine enjoying the boat or the welding right now, even if I wasn't so tired. The spark is just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still find no pleasure in your old activities even if your mood were to improve?\n\nPatient: If my mood improved? I mean, I guess if I felt better, maybe I'd want to do those things again. But right now it's just... I can't even imagine feeling better. It's like trying to picture what a house is gonna look like when you're just staring at the empty dirt lot, you know? Like when we did the HVAC on that new subdivision out by the highway. It was just acres of mud for months. We had to wear these massive rubber boots just to walk from the truck to the foundation, and the mud would just suck them right off your feet. I lost a good pair of insulated Wolverines out there once. Just stepped in a deep spot and the mud vacuumed it right off. I had to hop back to the truck with one sock on, freezing my foot off. Those boots were expensive, too. I bought them at this surplus store that used to be downtown, before all the gentrification happened. They turned that whole block into these overpriced loft apartments with exposed brick. They charge like three thousand a month for a place that used to be an old shoe factory. My aunt used to work in a shoe factory actually, back in the seventies. She said the smell of the leather and the glue gave her headaches every single day. She ended up quitting and becoming a dog groomer instead. She loved dogs. Had four poodles of her own... \n\nSorry. What was the question? If my mood improved? I don't know. Maybe. It's just hard to see that far ahead right now. I just feel so empty.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Harming myself? No, no, nothing like that. I mean, I've just been sitting around feeling empty, not wanting to die or anything. I don't think I could ever do that, honestly. I don't even like the sight of blood. Last time I had to get blood drawn for my physical, the phlebotomist couldn't find a vein. She kept digging around with the needle in my left arm, which is already sensitive because I got a bad sunburn on it last summer. I fell asleep in a lawn chair at my cousin's barbecue. He makes the best ribs, slow-cooks them over hickory wood for like eight hours. He built his own smoker out of an old oil drum. It's actually a pretty genius setup, he welded on these custom hinges and installed a temperature gauge right on the lid. The gauge he used is the same brand we install on some of the industrial HVAC units at work. Those things are heavy duty, built to withstand crazy temperature fluctuations. The last job site I was on, before I took some time off, we were installing ductwork in this massive commercial freezer facility. It was freezing in there, literally. You had to wear thermal gloves just to handle the sheet metal or your skin would stick to it. I hate wearing those bulky gloves, you lose all your dexterity. It's impossible to get a good grip on the tin snips...\n\nWait, sorry. Did you ask about hurting myself? Yeah, no. I haven't had any thoughts like that. Just... feeling really down and not wanting to do anything. But no thoughts of death.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find pleasure in only slightly or not at all?\n\nPatient: Activities I used to enjoy? Yeah, I mean... I used to love going out on the water. I have this little aluminum john boat I bought off a guy on Craigslist a few years ago. It needed a ton of work, the transom was completely rotted out. I had to replace the wood and seal it all up with marine-grade epoxy. That epoxy smells awful, by the way. It’s this two-part mixture, and if you don’t get the ratio exactly right, it never cures properly. It just stays tacky forever. I learned that the hard way when I tried to fix a fiberglass canoe for my brother. He ended up having to sand the whole thing down and start over. Sanding fiberglass is the absolute worst. The dust gets in your pores and makes you itch for days, even if you wear a Tyvek suit. Those suits are so hot, too. You wear one in the summer and you’re sweating buckets within five minutes. It’s like wearing a trash bag. I remember wearing one when we had to do some ductwork in a crawlspace under this old Victorian house. The place was full of spider webs and old newspapers from like, the 1940s. I actually found an old glass Coca-Cola bottle down there, the kind with the thick green glass. People collect those, you know. There's a whole market for vintage bottles...\n\nSorry, I'm rambling again. What did you ask? Things I used to enjoy? Yeah, the boat. And I used to do some metal sculpture in the garage, just welding scrap pieces together. But since my daughter left for basic training... I just don't see the point anymore. I haven't touched the boat or the welder. I come home to a dead house and I just can't keep myself together. I'm sitting in my truck crying before my shifts start. And at work, I'm missing steps on welding jobs I've done for years, just completely losing my focus. I've even been snapping at the younger guys on the crew, which isn't like me. I just... I don't care about the stuff I used to like doing.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Activities I still enjoy? I think you asked me that already, actually. But honestly, no. There's just nothing. I don't get pleasure out of anything right now. It's just... flat. Like a sheet of 24-gauge galvanized steel before it goes through the brake press. That machine is something else, by the way. It exerts tons of pressure, literally tons, just to bend the metal. You have to be so careful with your fingers. A guy I used to work with, Dave, he lost the tip of his index finger to one of those presses back in the nineties. He always joked about it, though. Said it made it easier to pick his nose. Dave was a character. He used to bring these massive, overstuffed meatball subs for lunch every single Friday. Got them from this little Italian place run by this old couple. The husband made the meatballs from scratch every morning at 4 AM. You could smell the garlic from halfway down the block. I tried making meatballs once, but I used too much breadcrumb and they turned out like hockey pucks. My dog wouldn't even eat them. He’s a golden retriever mix, eats literally everything, including a tube of chapstick once. I had to call poison control, and they just laughed and said he'd have very moisturized poops for a couple days... \n\nAnyway, sorry. No, I don't really find pleasure in anything at all right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would enjoy your old activities if you had the energy to do them?\n\nPatient: If I had the energy? I don't know. I mean, maybe? It's hard to even picture it right now. I just feel so drained all the time. Like when you run a battery down to absolutely zero, you know? Like those 18-volt lithium-ion batteries we use for the impact drivers at work. If you leave them out in the cold truck overnight, they just completely die on you. The cold just saps the life right out of them. My old foreman, he used to make us bring all the batteries into the job trailer at the end of the shift during the winter. He had this whole charging station set up, custom built it himself out of some scrap plywood and two-by-fours. He was a real stickler for organization. Every tool had its place, outlined in black marker on the pegboard. If you put a pair of Vice-Grips back in the wrong spot, you'd hear about it for a week. He was tough, but he knew his stuff. He retired a couple years ago, moved down to Florida. Bought a place near Tampa, I think. Or maybe it was Clearwater? Somewhere near the Gulf. I went down to the Gulf once, when I was in my twenties. The water is so warm there, like bathwater. Nothing like the ocean up here. Up here, you go in up to your knees and you can't feel your toes after five minutes...\n\nWhat were we talking about? Oh, right. Energy. Yeah, I don't know. I just... I can't imagine enjoying the boat or the welding right now, even if I wasn't so tired. The spark is just gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still find no pleasure in your old activities even if your mood were to improve?\n\nPatient: If my mood improved? I mean, I guess if I felt better, maybe I'd want to do those things again. But right now it's just... I can't even imagine feeling better. It's like trying to picture what a house is gonna look like when you're just staring at the empty dirt lot, you know? Like when we did the HVAC on that new subdivision out by the highway. It was just acres of mud for months. We had to wear these massive rubber boots just to walk from the truck to the foundation, and the mud would just suck them right off your feet. I lost a good pair of insulated Wolverines out there once. Just stepped in a deep spot and the mud vacuumed it right off. I had to hop back to the truck with one sock on, freezing my foot off. Those boots were expensive, too. I bought them at this surplus store that used to be downtown, before all the gentrification happened. They turned that whole block into these overpriced loft apartments with exposed brick. They charge like three thousand a month for a place that used to be an old shoe factory. My aunt used to work in a shoe factory actually, back in the seventies. She said the smell of the leather and the glue gave her headaches every single day. She ended up quitting and becoming a dog groomer instead. She loved dogs. Had four poodles of her own... \n\nSorry. What was the question? If my mood improved? I don't know. Maybe. It's just hard to see that far ahead right now. I just feel so empty.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would find pleasure in your old activities if you were doing them with someone you enjoy spending time with?\n\nPatient: With someone else? I... I really don't know. I haven't really wanted to be around anyone lately to even find out. I usually hang out with my buddy Sarah from the shop, we'd take the boat out or whatever, but she just had a baby anyway. A little boy, they named him Wyatt. Cute kid, but he screams like an absolute banshee. Sarah says he has colic. My mom always said I had colic when I was a baby, and she used to put a warm water bottle on my stomach to calm me down. Back then, they used those really thick red rubber water bottles that smelled weird when they got hot, you know? Kinda like burning tires. \n\nSpeaking of tires, I actually really need to get the alignment checked on my truck. It's been pulling hard to the right ever since I hit this massive crater of a pothole on the interstate last week. The roads around here are just absolute garbage after the winter. The salt and the plows just eat away at the asphalt until it looks like the surface of the moon. I blew out a tire on that exact same stretch of road two years ago and had to wait three hours for a tow truck because the cable holding my spare was completely rusted to the undercarriage. The tow guy had to get under there with a Sawzall just to cut it free. Sparks flying everywhere right next to the gas tank, which probably wasn't the smartest move, but he got it off...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Doing stuff with friends? Yeah, I just... I don't think it would make a difference right now. Even if Sarah was around, I just don't have it in me. I kind of just want to be left alone."
  }
